# Divergence ledger

Policy (agreed 2026-08-30): the strip and refactor phases are bug-for-bug
compatible with `main` so the comparison harness stays byte-exact. Every bug found
is recorded here, NOT fixed. Fixes happen in a dedicated phase after the refactor,
one commit per ledger entry, with regenerated goldens and the expected output
change documented in the commit.

Status values: OPEN (present in stripped code), REMOVED-BY-STRIP (the code
containing it was deleted in Phase 1), REMOVED-BY-REFACTOR (deleted by a
Phase 3 refactor stage), FIXED (dedicated fix commit merged).

| # | Location | Bug | Trigger / impact | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `src/ER_binary_func.cpp:78` (`fact()`) | Returns `int`; overflows at n ≥ 13, so `n_choose_r` is garbage for carrier counts k ≥ 13 | Can spuriously flip `IsExactVec` and activate the resampling branch in the efficient-resampling test; only reachable when `--max_MAC_for_ER` is raised above default 4 | FIXED (Phase 3e: n_choose_r computed via multiplicative double recurrence, INT_MAX-clamped — identical values for all k<=12; fact() deleted; accumulate overflow-guarded) |
| 2 | `src/Binary_resampling.cpp:49-56` (`SL_setseed`) | Ignores its seed argument in the R build (`_STAND_ALONE_` never defined); just calls `GetRNGstate()`. step2 never calls `set.seed`, and under `--nThreads>1` `mc.set.stream` unbinds `.Random.seed` in forked children → clock+pid seeding | If the resampling branch is ever reached (k ≥ 21, or k ≥ 13 via bug #1), step 2 results are nondeterministic | FIXED (Phase 3e: SL_setseed now seeds R RNG stream via set.seed before GetRNGstate, making the resampling branch reproducible; unreached at defaults so goldens unchanged. **Re-checked in Phase 4 C2**, which makes `--nThreads > 1` actually fork: the fork path is deterministic. `R/MarkerShard.R` calls `set.seed(1)` in the parent and forks with `mc.set.seed = FALSE`, so every child inherits one fixed stream rather than a pid/clock-derived one. Gated by golden P1 (the ER path, `--minMAC=1`) reproducing byte-identically at `nThreads` ∈ {1,2,3,4,7,64} and by three repeats at `nThreads=4` being mutually identical. **Amended 2026-09-02 (S2):** the 3e fix re-seeded at EVERY `ComputeExact::Run` entry, which meant every ER marker entered the R API (`Rcpp::Environment` -> `set.seed` -> `GetRNGstate`) whether or not it drew anything -- the hazard that blocks threading the marker loop (F4 in `docs/design/step2-threading-verdict.md`). The call is now conditional on some `m_IsExact[i] != 1`, i.e. on a resampling arm actually running, which is byte-neutral because the arms that draw still re-seed with 1 at entry and the exact recursions consume no randomness at all. Measured by tracing `base::set.seed` with both builds installed side by side: 2 -> 0 calls on P1's configuration (default `max_MAC_for_ER=4`, 2 ER markers, both exact) and 3 -> 1 on P33's (`--max_MAC_for_ER=200`, 3 ER markers of which 1 resamples), with all four runs byte-identical to their goldens) |
| 3 | `src/Main.cpp:2256-2270` | `char* end_prev` declared uninitialised; for `t_genoType=="vcf"` neither assigning branch runs, then `std::remove(end_prev)` executes on it — undefined behavior | Current VCF region path is UB; do not use its outputs as a behavioral reference | REMOVED-BY-STRIP (Strip 5). The sibling `std::remove(end)` calls on plink/bgen parse pointers remain — cstdio remove("") no-ops, preserved bug-for-bug |
| 4 | `R/Util.R:273-316` vs `R/Geno.R:11-20,538-560` | `checkIfSampleIDsExist` and `getSampleIDsFromBGEN` defined verbatim twice; no `Collate:` field so C-locale file order decides — the `Util.R` copies win | Byte-equivalent today, so no behavior change; footgun if either copy is edited. Strip deletes both copies | REMOVED-BY-STRIP (Util.R copies in Strip 1, Geno.R copies with BGEN in Strip 6) |
| 5 | `src/Main.hpp:8-16` | Declares `setAssocTest_GlobalVarsInCPP` with 9 params; definition in `Main.cpp:141-151` has 11. Header is stale and never-matching | No runtime impact (header only included by Main.cpp/LDmat.cpp, Rcpp generates its own decls); documents that Main.hpp cannot be trusted as a contract | FIXED (Phase 3e: declarations aligned to definitions — setAssocTest_GlobalVarsInCPP gains t_MACCutoffforER, fast_logistf_fit gains isfirthconverge, dead case-typo writeOutfile_singleinGroup decl removed) |
| 6 | `src/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.cpp` PCG loops (e.g. `:5010-5017`) | PCG stop rule `sum(rVec % rVec) > tolPCG` is an absolute float threshold with no ‖b‖ normalisation; `tolPCG=1e-5` against a float accumulation over N elements is loose | Step-1 numerics only pinned to ~1e-3–1e-2; a last-bit change near threshold shifts iteration count by one. Constrains harness tolerances (not a correctness bug per se) | **DECIDED 2026-09-05: leave, documented as a potential problem.** Options laid out for the owner were leave / normalise the stop rule by the right-hand side norm; the owner chose to leave it and record the hazard. Recorded in `docs/state/next.md`. What a future fix would move, traced 2026-09-05: every sparse-GRM step-1 golden bypasses the PCG loop entirely (`getPCG1ofSigmaAndVector` takes the `gen_spsolve_v4` branch when `isUseSparseSigmaforModelFitting && !isUsePCGwithSparseSigma`, which is every `--useSparseGRMtoFitNULL=TRUE` run at the default `--usePCGwithSparseGRM=FALSE`), so only `binary_denseGRM` and its pgen mirror would move, and no step-2 golden consumes them. [ASSUMPTION: traced through the branch, not instrumented -- the first step of any fix is a perturbed-tolPCG drill to confirm exactly which goldens move.] The hazard for a real analysis is at biobank N with a dense GRM, where the unnormalised residual sum grows with N and a fixed 1e-5 becomes a relatively looser stop. |

BETA and SE are unchanged, so no golden moves (P31 is at cutoff 0.05, where no marker drifts). With `length(tempPos) == 1` the selection degrades to a bare numeric, and `get_SKAT_pvalue`'s `BETA_Burden = sum(Score)/(sum(diag(Phi)))` (`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:381`) then hits the `diag()` scalar branch: `diag(x)` for a length-1 numeric builds a `trunc(x)`-square **identity matrix**, so for a variance below 1 it is 0 x 0 and `sum()` is `0` | **Silent `Inf` in `BETA_Burden_cond` / `SE_Burden_cond` beside a correct p-value**, exit status 0, no warning. Verified against the pinned env's SKAT rather than inferred: `Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue` **accepts** the bare scalar and returns the right p-value (scalar and 1 x 1 both give p = 0.008711913), so there is NO `try-error` and NO NA rescue — the plausible assumption that the error branch masks this is wrong. `BETA_Burden` is `Inf` on the scalar route vs 4.047619 on the 1 x 1 route, and `SE_Burden = abs(Inf/qnorm(p/2))` is `Inf` too. **The `Inf` is only the small-variance corner.** `diag()` on a length-1 numeric reads it as a DIMENSION, so it *constructs* a `trunc(x)`-square identity and the allocation grows as x^2: `diag(2.5)` is 2 x 2 (denominator 2, a plausible-looking wrong beta), `diag(625)` is 3 Mb, and `diag(4e5)` requests **1.2 Tb** and aborts the run with "vector memory limit of 16.0 Gb reached". The argument here is a *weighted score variance*, so large values are the normal case, not a corner: the bundled quantitative conditional marker golden already carries variances up to 625 on a 1000-sample toy fixture, and `wadjVarSMat_cond = VarMat * weightMat` multiplies that by weights whose beta(1,25) values reach ~25 (so ~625 squared) -- and the variance itself scales with N, so biobank-scale input pushes the requested allocation far past any machine. Net: below 1 the run silently emits `Inf` betas and continues; at realistic magnitudes it **hard-crashes on allocation**, losing the whole job, with an out-of-memory message that names neither the region nor the cause. The only guard on the cell is `length(tempPos) > 0` (`:335`), so a single-marker anno x max_MAF cell (a gene with one LoF variant at `max_MAF=0.0001`) is routine. Path: quantitative trait + `--condition` + K = 1 + `--r.corr=0`. No bundled golden covers it — there is no conditional quantitative region run. Latent sibling, same cause: `:249` `outList$VarMatAdjCond[noNAIndices, noNAIndices]` also lacks `drop = FALSE`; a one-marker region collapses it to a scalar and `:449`'s `[tempPos, tempPos, drop = F]` then raises "incorrect number of dimensions". Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit; pinned by `tests/testthat/test-unit-region-cond.R` | FIXED, in two layers. The *call sites* gained `drop = FALSE` (`:464`, and the latent sibling `:249`), but the real fix is at the landmine: `get_SKAT_pvalue` now takes the trace as `varSum_Phi = if (is.matrix(Phi)) sum(diag(Phi)) else sum(Phi)`, so `diag()` can never be handed a length-1 numeric by ANY caller, present or future. Fixing only the call sites would have left the quadratic allocation armed. Shape by shape the replacement is byte-identical on every reachable path -- a matrix still gives the trace, a bare vector still gives `sum(Phi)`, which is exactly what `sum(diag(vector))` computed -- so only the scalar case changes, from `0` (hence `Inf`) or a `trunc(x)`-square identity to the value itself, and nothing is allocated. Full golden cycle **byte-identical**, as required: no reachable path passes a scalar today, which is precisely why this was latent rather than observed. Characterization test inverted in the same commit and extended to assert the scalar and 1 x 1 routes now agree on every statistic, that a realistic weighted variance (4e5) is traced rather than allocated, and that matrix/vector inputs are untouched |
| 35 | `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2268` | `for(i in 1:(numCate-1))` where `numCate = length(cateVarRatioIndexVec)`, defaulting to `rep(1, length(cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude))` (`:2266-2267`). With a single MAC category `1:0` is `c(1, 0)`, so the loop runs twice and pass two evaluates `listOfMarkersForVarRatio[[0]] = ...` (`:2270`) | Step 1 **crashes** with "attempt to select less than one element" under `--isCateVarianceRatio=TRUE` with a length-1 `--cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude`. The option is a free-form comma string (`extdata/step1_fitNULLGLMM.R:301`) and nothing in `R/checkArgs.R` or `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R` requires length >= 2 (verified). `seq_len(numCate-1)` is empty and correct. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit; the `1:0` idiom is pinned by `tests/testthat/test-unit-type-polymorphism.R` | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: `seq_len(numCate - 1)`. Gated by `tests/testthat/test-step1-guards.R` (standard): a single-category `--cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude=20.5` with `--isCateVarianceRatio=TRUE` fits and writes one categorical ratio, where HEAD died with "attempt to select less than one element". The `1:0` idiom pin in `test-unit-type-polymorphism.R` pins R, not this code, and stands |
| 36 | `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:1636` (`Covariate_Transform`), also `R/SAIGE_extractNeff.R:406` | `X1 <- X1[, -idx.na]` omits `drop = FALSE`, so dropping all but one design column yields a bare vector; `N <- nrow(X1)` (`:1650`) is then `NULL` and `X1_new <- X1_Q * sqrt(N)` (`:1653`) dies | Step 1 crashes with "non-numeric argument to mathematical function", a message pointing nowhere near the cause. Reached when `ncol(model.matrix) - length(idx.na) == 1`, i.e. all but one design column collinear; the cheapest route is a **quantitative** trait with a single constant covariate, since the one-covariate short-circuit at `:1024` is `traitType == "binary"`-only and `checkPerfectSep` (`:2120`) is binary-only too. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: `X1[, -idx.na, drop = FALSE]`. Gated by `test-step1-guards.R`: a quantitative fit whose only covariate is a derived constant column drops it with the collinearity warning and continues, where HEAD died in "non-numeric argument to mathematical function" |
| 37 | `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:962`, same pattern at `R/SAIGE_extractNeff.R:347` | `seq(1, nrow(mmat_nomissing), by = 1)` raises "wrong sign in 'by' argument" when the frame is empty, so the intended `stop("No individuals left in the analysis. Exiting...")` immediately below (`:965-967`) is **dead code that can never fire** | The user gets `seq.default`'s diagnostic instead of the written explanation. Trigger: a `--SampleIDIncludeFile` disjoint from the phenotype file (`:888`); the `--FemaleOnly` / `--MaleOnly` filters at `:913` / `:922` have their own working `nrow == 0` guards. Cosmetic — no computation affected. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**, in two places: `seq_len(nrow(...))` at both sites (`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R`, `R/SAIGE_extractNeff.R`), and the "No individuals left in the analysis" stop moved to where the `--SampleIDIncludeFile` subset is applied -- because with factor covariates a 0-row frame died in `model.matrix`'s `contrasts<-` before ever reaching the original site, so fixing the `seq()` alone would still have hidden the message. Gated by `test-step1-guards.R`: a disjoint include file gets the message, and neither `seq.default`'s nor `contrasts<-`'s |
| 38 | `R/SAIGE_Test_main.R:332-334` | `X_PARregion_mat = rbind(X_PARregion_mat, as.numeric(unlist(strsplit(X_PARregion_list[lxp], split = "-"))))` — the appended row is whatever width `strsplit` returned, and `rbind` recycles silently. A token with no `-` gives length 1, so `rbind(<1x2 matrix>, 5)` becomes the row `c(5, 5)`; if the *first* token is malformed the matrix is 1-wide and the next `rbind` hard-errors on column mismatch instead | **Silently wrong pseudo-autosomal boundaries**, with no warning: the result goes straight to `setAssocTest_GlobalVarsInCPP_X_PARregion_mat()`. There is no validation of `X_PARregion` anywhere — `extdata/step2_SPAtests.R:290` is a free-form character option. Gated on `--is_rewrite_XnonPAR_forMales=TRUE`, so unreached at defaults. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: each `--X_PARregion` token must be `start-end`, numeric, `start <= end`, or the run stops naming the token. Gated by `tests/testthat/test-step2-parregion.R` (standard): a dash-less token, a non-numeric end and a reversed range are each refused by name; the default string is exercised by `test-step2-xnonpar-chrom.R`. The rbind-recycling pin in `test-unit-type-polymorphism.R` pins R and stands |
| 39 | `R/readInGLMM.R:405-414` (`Get_Variance_Ratio`) | `ratioVec_null_noXadj <- as.numeric(ratioVec_null_noXadj)` sits in the `else` branch of `if(length(ratioVec_null_noXadj) > 0)`, so the coercion runs **only when the vector is empty** and can never do anything; the sibling ratios at `:386` and `:396` *are* converted. The `stop("categorical variance Ratio null_noXadj are needed")` guard beside it (`:411`) is unreachable for the same reason — it tests `length == 1` inside the zero-length branch | Logic inversion. The `null_noXadj` ratios are passed downstream with whatever type `fread` inferred for column 1, unlike every other ratio vector: any row that makes that column character (a stray quoted value) sends variance ratios into `setSAIGEobjInCPP` as strings. Adjacent to and compounding LEDGER #13, whose `numeric(0)` pass-through was the mechanism behind #10. Found 2026-08-31 during the type-polymorphic-builtin audit | **FIXED (2026-09-05)**: the coercion and the cardinality guard moved into the populated branch, where the sibling ratios have them. Gated by a block in `tests/testthat/test-unit-varianceratio.R` (fast): categorical `null` rows with a single `null_noXadj` row now stop with "categorical variance Ratio null_noXadj are needed" (the guard was unreachable before), and a well-formed file yields numeric ratios. Every committed ratio file is numeric, so no golden can move |

## Type-polymorphic idiom inventory (audit, 2026-08-31)

Entries #33-#39 came out of a sweep for base-R functions whose *semantics*, not
just their result, change with the type/length/shape of the argument. The
remaining sites are latent — each is unreachable today for a stated reason — but
they are the same hazard and are recorded so a future change does not
un-guard one silently. Verified in the pinned env:

| call | result |
|---|---|
| `diag(m)`, `m` q x q | length-q diagonal (the intended use) |
| `diag(m)`, `m` q x 1 | **first element only**, silently |
| `diag(v)`, `v` length q > 1 | q x q diagonal **matrix** |
| `diag(x)`, `x` length-1 numeric < 1 | **0 x 0 matrix**; `sum()` is `0` |
| `diag(x)`, `x` length-1 numeric >= 1 | `trunc(x)` x `trunc(x)` **identity** |
| `sample(x, n)`, `x` length 1 | samples from `1:x`, **not** from `x` |
| `1:n`, `n == 0` | `c(1, 0)` — the loop runs twice, backwards |

**`sample()` on an index vector that can have length 1.**
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2270` and `:2279` pass `which(MACdata$MACvector > ...)`
straight to `sample()`. A MAC category holding exactly one marker becomes
`sample(47L, 1)` — a uniform draw from `1:47`, i.e. a *different, wrong* marker
silently substituted into the variance-ratio set. Unreachable only because the
`length(...) < numMarkers` check at `:2313` (default 30) aborts the run first;
`--numRandomMarkerforVarianceRatio=1` removes that guard. Same shape at
`R/SAIGE_createSparseGRM.R:89`, where the guard at `:79` allows equality and
`--numRandomMarkerforSparseKin` has no lower bound. `:2261` is harmless **by
accident**: its `MACindex` is `1:nrow(MACdata)`, so the degenerate case is
`sample(1, 1)`, which returns the right answer. `sample.int(length(x))` used as
an index is the idiom that cannot fail.

**`1:n` with a reachable `n == 0`.** Beyond #35 and #37:
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2250` (`seq(1, 0)` silently gives `c(1, 0)`, then a
differing-rows error); `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1526`, where a non-integral
`ngroup` additionally truncates a partial region without warning;
`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1530` `3:length(...)` (guarded by `checkGroupFile`);
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:1964-1965` `seq(1:Nval)`, the literal anti-pattern,
doubly wrong at `Nval == 0`; `R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:954` `2:(ncol(mmat)-1)`,
which *reverses* to `c(2, 1)`. Correctly guarded, for contrast:
`R/checkArgs.R:135` and `:144`, `R/Geno.R:137` and `:175`,
`R/SAIGE_Test_main.R:589`, `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1626`.

**`class(x) == "..."`.** `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:382` was the only one in
the tree. Safe as written (both reachable classes are length 1) but a hard `if`
error on R >= 4.2 the moment a length-2 class appears — `class(matrix(1))` is
`c("matrix", "array")`. Converted to `inherits()` in the audit commit as a
byte-neutral change, matching the idiom already used at `R/MarkerShard.R:218`.

**`if (cond)` where `cond` can be `NA`.** `R/Util.R:103` uses non-short-circuiting
`|` across three comparisons, so a truncated `.index` file (an interrupted run)
makes the whole expression `NA` and raises "missing value where TRUE/FALSE
needed" instead of the intended "modified by user" message. `R/Util.R:224` is
the same shape and is already covered by #23.

**`stringsAsFactors`.** `DESCRIPTION` declares `R (>= 3.5.0)`, where bare
`data.frame()` / `read.table()` still factorise: `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1555`,
`R/SAIGE_createSparseGRM.R:52`, `R/Util.R:101`, `R/Geno.R:224`. All are rescued
today because their consumers go via labels (`as.matrix.data.frame`, `gsub`,
`write.table`) — latent on R >= 4.0, live on the declared minimum. The pattern
to copy is `suppressWarnings(as.numeric(...))` followed by an `anyNA` check, as
at `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:215` and `R/SAIGE_Test_main.R:21`.

**Safe `diag()` sites, recorded so they are not "fixed".**
`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region_Func.R:250` `diag(as.matrix(Phi))` — the `as.matrix()`
is the correct defence and is exactly what `:381` lacks (note it would still
truncate silently if `Phi` ever arrived as a length-q *vector*).
`R/Firth.R:334` — `chol()` always returns a matrix (`dim(chol(4))` is `1 1`), so
the log-determinant is right even for a scalar argument.
`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:2059` — a `Matrix` method.
`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:264` `VarSVec = diag(outList$VarMat)` — safe
(`VarMat` is an `arma::mat`, so it always carries a `dim`, 1 x 1 included) and
also a **dead assignment**: `VarSVec` is written at `:264-265` and never read
anywhere in the tree.

**Dead code carrying the same hazards** (no callers in `R/`, `src/`, `extdata/`
or `tests/`; all package-exported via `NAMESPACE`'s `exportPattern`, so deleting
them is a public-API change and out of scope for the audit): the whole of
`R/Firth.R` (Firth is implemented in C++ and reached via `--is_Firth_beta`;
`test-unit-firth.R` exercises the Rcpp export, not this file);
`pcg` / `pcgSparse` (`R/SAIGE_fitGLMM_fast.R:1673`, `:1723`), which contain the
textbook `dA <- diag(A)` at `:1676` and `:1726` plus an unconditional debug
`print("R"); print(Minv)` at `:1681-1682`; `bigGRMPar` / `bigGRMPar_new` /
`refineKinPar`; `ReadGroupFile`; `Util.R::writeOutputFile`;
`checkArgsBool` / `checkArgsNumeric`; and the entire
`regionTestType == "BURDEN"` arm of the R post-processing layer (see #33 for
why it is unreachable).

**Searched for and absent:** no live `sapply` / `apply` / `mapply` (only in the
dead `bigGRMPar*` and commented-out lines), no `which.max` / `which.min`, no
`nchar`, no integer-overflow-prone `sum()`. `vapply` is used correctly with an
explicit `FUN.VALUE`, and every one of the five `merge()` call sites is followed
by an explicit re-`order()` on an index column. The character promotion in the
Cauchy row (`R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:531-569`) is deliberate and documented
in-file as golden-load-bearing — it is not a defect.

## Ledger entries #40 onwards

Same table as the one at the top of this file; it continues here because the
type-polymorphic audit above was inserted between them. **The numbering is one
sequence -- #1-#39 above, #40 onwards below** -- so search this file by number,
not by table. (Before 2026-09-04 these rows had no header and no delimiter row,
so no markdown renderer drew them and the ledger appeared to stop at #39.)

| # | Location | Bug | Trigger / impact | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 40 | `src/SAIGE_test.cpp` `SAIGEClass::getMarkerPval`: the conditional-analysis SPA block (`if(t_isCondition)` ... `if(m_traitType != "quantitative" && stat_c > pow(m_SPA_Cutoff,2))`) vs the marginal SPA block (`if(!t_isER)` ... `if(!isnan(StdStat) && StdStat > m_SPA_Cutoff && m_traitType != "quantitative")`) | The conditional block reads `m1`, `p_iIndexComVecSize`, `NAmu`, `NAsigma`, `tol1` and the four vectors `gNA`/`gNB`/`muNA`/`muNB`, but **only the marginal block assigns them**, under a *different* predicate. The conditional block sits OUTSIDE the `if(!t_isER){...}else{...}` pair that encloses the marginal one, so it runs whether or not the marginal block did. When the marginal branch is skipped, all five doubles are read uninitialised (`double q, qinv, m1, NAmu, NAsigma, tol1, p_iIndexComVecSize;` with no initialiser) and the vectors are read sized-but-uninitialised — undefined behaviour, and `p_iIndexComVecSize` is itself the predicate selecting `SPA_fast` vs `SPA` | Reachable in ordinary conditional analysis of a **binary** trait: any marker whose marginal \|Z\| <= `SPAcutoff` (or which took the ER path, `t_isER == true`) but whose conditional statistic exceeds the cutoff — i.e. exactly the markers conditioning strengthens, which is a normal outcome, not a corner. The garbage `p_iIndexComVecSize` then picks the SPA variant at random, and `m1`/`NAmu`/`NAsigma`/`tol1` feed the saddlepoint directly. Quantitative traits are immune (the block is gated on `m_traitType != "quantitative"`), which is why golden P4 (the only conditional MARKER golden, quantitative) does not reach it; no binary conditional single-variant golden exists. Found 2026-08-31 while implementing Phase 4 C3, by mapping the brace structure to check the plan's caveat about deferring the `gNA`/`gNB`/`muNA`/`muNB` allocations. **Not verified by execution** — [ASSUMPTION: reachability derived from the control-flow structure, not from an observed wrong answer; the read is UB so its effect is not predictable]. C3 defers the four vector allocations out of the per-marker path but deliberately re-sizes them at the conditional use site, so the state that block sees is bit-for-bit what it saw before | FIXED. **The [ASSUMPTION] is now an OBSERVATION, and the trigger was not the one anticipated.** A behaviour-neutral detector (a bool plus one stderr line, no value altered) was built FIRST, purely to ask whether the path executes on real data. It does: **2 of 70 markers on the bundled binary fixture under an ordinary `--condition`**, in every conditioning set tried. But the trigger is the **ER path**, not the "marginal \|Z\| below cutoff" route this entry emphasised: the hits report `isER=1` with `StdStat` 3.94 and 2.63, i.e. **above** the SPA cutoff, so these are markers that would otherwise have been SPA-corrected -- `if(!t_isER)` skips the whole marginal block including its assignments. Fix: a `prepSPAContext()` lambda computes the shared context (`t_gtilde`, `p_iIndexComVecSize`, `m1`, the `p >= 0.5` fills, `NAmu`, `NAsigma`, `tol1`); the conditional block calls it and it is a no-op whenever the marginal block already ran, so that path is untouched and any output change is attributable to the fix alone. C3's four `set_size` calls are removed -- they existed only to reproduce the sized-but-uninitialised state, and since `set_size` does not preserve contents they would silently wipe data on a future size mismatch instead of failing loudly. **Impact measured**: exactly 2 rows change per run, the same 2 the detector flagged. rs44 `p.value_c` 7.81467e-05 -> 0.002349397, a **30x shift in the ANTI-CONSERVATIVE direction** (a spuriously significant conditional association); rs56 0.008495285 -> 0.006259919. `BETA_c` is untouched, as expected -- it does not depend on the SPA context. Every pre-existing golden is byte-identical, which is the FINDING rather than a pass: nothing frozen covered this path, exactly as this entry predicted. **Golden P12 `plink_marker_cond_binary.txt` added** to close that gap; it pins the corrected values and would have differed under the buggy build |
| 41 | `src/SAIGE_test.cpp:950` (`SAIGEClass::getMarkerPval`, conditional-analysis SPA block) against its own SE at `:929` | The conditional SPA p-value is **printed halved**: `sprintf(pValueBuf_SPA_c, "%.6E", SPApval_c/2)`, while the SE written beside it is built from the UN-halved value (`t_qval_c = quantile(ns, SPApval_c/2)` -> `t_seBeta_c = fabs(t_Beta_c)/t_qval_c`, `:929-934`). The `/2` belongs to the two-sided normal quantile, not to the p-value, and was copied into the print. Regression, dated exactly: the pre-image was `t_pval_c = SPApval_c;` (a double), and `c032c687` (2022-08-17, "format p-values in single-variant assoc tests so very small p-values will not be 0") converted it to a formatted string and carried the `/2` across. `c032c687` is an ancestor of the `v1.1.5` tag commit `f6564bd9` (same day), so **every SAIGE from 1.1.5 onward carries it**; 1.1.4 and earlier do not. The marginal block one screen up prints `t_SPApval` un-halved (`:731`), and the log-p arm of this same conditional block prints `SPApval_c` un-halved (`:959`), so the column is not even self-consistent: a marker whose conditional p underflows to the log-p format is reported correctly while its neighbour is reported at half | **Every reported conditional p-value from the SPA path is 2x anti-conservative.** Trigger: binary trait + `--condition`, any marker whose conditional score statistic clears `SPAcutoff^2` and whose SPA converges — the ordinary case, not a corner. Signature (the geometry that started the hunt -- a user's `p.value` vs `2*pnorm(-abs(BETA/SE))` scatter, which turned out to be the MARGINAL ancestor of this bug, fixed upstream in 2023; see the historical note below): the points split into the identity line plus a **slope-1/2 branch** below the SPA gate, i.e. the printed p equals the ONE-sided `pnorm(-abs(BETA_c/SE_c))`. Measured on the bundled binary fixture (`--condition=rs13,rs79`, 70 markers): **4/70 halved at the default `SPAcutoff=2`, 44/70 at `SPAcutoff=0.5`** — the count tracks the gate, which is the positive control; the ratio is exactly 0.5 or exactly 1.0 with nothing in between. Negative controls: quantitative traits are immune (block gated on `m_traitType != "quantitative"`) — 0/32 in P4/P16; and the MARGINAL `p.value` round-trips exactly under every flag combination tried (default, `SPAcutoff=0.5`, `--is_Firth_beta` with `--pCutoffforFirth=0.1`, `--condition`), so this is specific to the conditional column and not a misreading of the round-trip identity. **Not confined to the printed marker column.** `pval_c` is a `std::string` end to end (`src/Main.cpp:191`), and the `--r.corr=1` BURDEN path parses it straight back with `std::stod` (`src/Main.cpp:2019`) into `CCT_cpp`, so gene-level `Pvalue_Burden_c` **and the region's Cauchy row inherit the halving**: on a BURDEN-only conditional run of the bundled fixture (`--is_fastTest=FALSE`, LEDGER #27), GENE1's Cauchy `Pvalue_Burden_c` is 0.0512759, exactly `CCT` over the halved cells, against 0.0755848 over the corrected ones (at `SPAcutoff=0.5`: 0.0329142 vs 0.0669001). The SKAT-O (`--r.corr=0`) region statistics are computed in R from the score/variance matrices and are NOT affected — all 12 non-Cauchy rows of golden P3 round-trip at ratio exactly 1. Affected committed goldens: `{plink,pgen}_marker_cond_binary` 4/70 rows each, `{plink,pgen}_group_cond_binary.singleAssoc` 1/19, `{plink,pgen}_group_cond_multiweight.singleAssoc` 3/31 (P12/P3/P10 and their PGEN mirrors); P4/P16 (quantitative) are clean. **This entry corrects LEDGER #40's impact figure**: the post-fix rs44 value it quotes, `p.value_c` 0.002349397, is itself a halved print — the un-halved SPA p is 0.004698794, so #40's fix moved rs44 from 7.81467e-05 to 4.70e-03, a **60x** anti-conservative shift, not the 30x recorded there. Found 2026-09-01. Fix when the phase comes: drop the `/2` at `:950` only — `:929` is correct and must not be touched, and the SE is therefore already right, so a regenerated golden should show `p.value_c` doubled on exactly the affected rows with `BETA_c`/`SE_c`/`Tstat_c`/`var_c` byte-identical. Every conditional golden moves, which is the point; note also that any published conditional analysis run with SAIGE >= 1.1.5 is affected in the same direction. **Validated at scale 2026-09-01** on the largest bundled fixture (`nfam_100_nindep_0_step1_includeMoreRareVariants_poly_22chr`, 128,868 markers x 1,000 samples, y_binary 98/902, `--condition=rs10325,rs99612 --SPAcutoff=1.6449`): **10,085 of 128,868 markers (7.83%) printed halved**, ratio exactly 0.5 or exactly 1.0 with nothing between (max deviation 6.4e-5 = the 6-significant-figure print), while the MARGINAL column of the same file round-trips at 1. The candidate fix (drop `/2` at `:950`, rebuild) was then run on the same command: **every column except `p.value_c` is byte-identical**, `p.value_c` is exactly doubled on the affected rows (median ratio 2.000000, range 1.999999-2.000001) and untouched (ratio exactly 1) everywhere else, and the round-trip is restored across all 128,868 rows. The decisive check is distributional: SPA-p / score-p is **0.9811** (IQR 0.956-0.995) in the marginal column, **0.4903** (IQR 0.478-0.497) in the conditional column as printed, and **0.9806** after the fix -- the saddlepoint correction cannot halve a p-value on the conditional statistic when it multiplies by 0.98 on the marginal statistic of the same markers and trait. Cost at this scale: 10 markers reach conditional p < 1e-4 as printed, 7 after the fix. The buggy tree was restored and the restore verified twice (re-run output byte-identical to the pre-experiment run; golden P1 byte-identical) | FIXED (`src/SAIGE_test.cpp:950`). The print now emits `SPApval_c`; the two-sided quantile at `:929` that builds `t_seBeta_c` is untouched, because it was already right -- so **no statistic moves, only the printed conditional p-value**. The blast radius was predicted before regenerating and matched exactly: 6 golden files, 8 rows, one column. `{plink,pgen}_marker_cond_binary.txt` 4 rows each, `{plink,pgen}_group_cond_binary.txt.singleAssoc.txt` 1 each, `{plink,pgen}_group_cond_multiweight.txt.singleAssoc.txt` 3 each; `p.value_c` is the ONLY column that differs in any of them, every changed value is **exactly doubled**, and the BETA_c/SE_c round trip is now 1 on every row of every conditional golden. P4/P16 (quantitative conditional) and the SKAT-O region columns are untouched, as predicted -- the block is gated on `traitType != "quantitative"` and the region statistics come from R. **The coverage gap is closed in the same commit**: goldens **P25/P26** `{plink,pgen}_group_cond_burden.txt` pin `--r.corr=1` together with `--condition`, the combination through which the halved string reached a GENE-level statistic. Nothing frozen covered it -- P3/P10 are conditional but SKAT-O, P5/P9 are BURDEN but unconditional -- which is exactly why #41 sat inside every conditional golden without any of them failing. On that new golden the fix moves GENE1's Cauchy conditional p from **0.0497081 to 0.0721069**: the buggy build called the region conditionally significant at the conventional 0.05 and the fixed one does not. MEASURED across all 70 markers on the P30 configuration: the two covariate-collinear markers give 1.7e-16 and 4.2e-16 (machine epsilon, total cancellation) and EVERY other marker gives >= 0.237 -- 14.8 orders of separation, with the threshold ~500x above the cancellation and ~12 orders below the smallest real marker. Under #106's relative rule the solve converges and returns the true, genuinely tiny variance (6.84797e-29 for rs20, ~279 orders of magnitude ABOVE `DBL_MIN`), so P27's two rows printed `BETA=-4.37022e+13 SE=1.20842e+14 p=7.176163E-01` -- the original #44 defect, restored by a fix elsewhere, and WORSE than the first time: a 4.4e13 entry in the `BETA` column raises `rel_err()`'s column-scaled absolute floor by 14 orders and would have masked the other 68 markers of that column in numeric mode. `varianceCollapsed` cannot catch it, because there is no cancellation here: `P2Vec` and `gtilde` are both ~1e-16 componentwise and every term of `var2` has the same sign -- it is small because its INPUT is nothing. So `projectionCollapsed` (`src/SAIGE_test.cpp`, next to `varianceCollapsed`) asks the question one level up: did the covariate projection annihilate the genotype? `gtilde` is `g - X (X'WX)^-1 X'W g`, whose componentwise rounding error is bounded by about `n*eps` times the scale of `g`, so `\|\|gtilde\|\|^2 <= (n*eps)^2 \|\|g\|\|^2` says "what is left of `g` after the projection is entirely roundoff". Scale-free by construction -- a ratio of two norms of the same vector's scale -- and it costs two dot products per marker against a PCG solve. MEASURED on the P27 configuration, all 70 markers: rs20 and rs22 give 9.1e-31 and 7.5e-31; EVERY other marker gives >= 0.328. That is 29.5 orders of separation, with the threshold `(n*eps)^2 = 4.9e-26` sitting ~5e4 above the collapse and ~25 orders below the smallest real marker. Both guards are OR-ed into one `collapsed` predicate driving the `p = 1` branch and the `NA` branch. Every duplicate was already present in the pre-kill file and NO marker is lost, so the direction is duplication, never truncation -- benign for a scan you re-run, corrupting for anything that joins or counts rows. **Pre-existing and orthogonal to threading**: the serial restart (`nThreadsMarker=1`, `nThreads=1`) and the in-process restart (`nThreadsMarker=4`) produce BYTE-IDENTICAL 132,695-row files, which is also the parity evidence for S7. That order is the fix: without the flush the recorded count would omit the chunk's own tail and the restart would discard good rows. An index written before this (no byte count) still restarts, with a warning that rows may be duplicated, rather than being refused. Gated by a RECONSTRUCTED interruption in `tests/testthat/test-step2-threads.R` -- a complete run's output truncated to a point past a recorded chunk boundary, which is the same state a kill leaves but deterministic -- asserting the restart reproduces the complete run byte for byte; drill-verified by disabling `truncateOutputToIndex`, which fails it. HISTORY (the row as it stood while OPEN): **do not "fix" without reading this.** Making the region path read the flag would preserve no goldens (the screen would use a different variance ratio AND a different score test, so near-cutoff genes could fail to escalate) and the prize is bounded at **8%**: the same 210 genes with `scoreTestFast` throughout (no sparse GRM, so `useSparseGRM` false everywhere) cost 147.7 s against 161.1 s. The defensible change is to WARN that `--is_fastTest` does nothing for `--groupFile` runs except cost time, not to implement the missing half. **That warning landed 2026-09-04** (`R/SAIGE_Test_main.R`, beside the existing Firth/r.corr warning, where `is_fastTest` is final -- after the conditional path has forced it FALSE). The entry stays OPEN because the underlying asymmetry is unchanged and is upstream's; only the silence is fixed. `--pval_cutoff_for_fastTest` reached the CLI in the same commit, which is what made golden **P35** possible -- P2's command with the cutoff at 1, so both regions take the ESCALATION pass. Its bytes equal P2's, which IS this entry: the second pass recomputes what the first already had. The golden pins the other WRITER, not the numbers, and under LEDGER #50 it lost all 19 single-variant rows while P2 stayed green. Wasted work, right answer -- the #49 shape **Reported upstream: https://github.com/saigegit/SAIGE/issues/214** |

Found 2026-09-03 while adding PGEN input to steps 0/1, which match phenotypes on IID and so would have hit it first. | **FIXED**: `readPsamFile` now finds the `IID` column by name from the single-`#` header line (mirroring `R/Geno.R`), strips `\r`, skips blank lines, refuses a psam with no header / no `IID` column / a short row, and **refuses a duplicate IID** rather than overwriting it -- the overwrite was the mechanism of the undercount. Drill-verified: **6 failures at unpatched HEAD, 0 after**, with the verbatim and no-FID cases passing in BOTH builds -- which is what isolates the FID != IID part from the rewrite. At unpatched HEAD the duplicate-IID case exits 0 with no warning, i.e. the undercount really is silent. (b) That 1 is not inert: BOTH CCT implementations detect `any(p == 1)` and return `min(1, n * min(p))` instead of the Cauchy combination -- a deliberate, documented modification (`R/CCT_modified.R:1`) -- so ONE saturated cell reroutes the WHOLE region's `Cauchy` row to a Bonferroni over its cells while the column keeps its name | **Reproduced by hand (2026-09-04), on the golden that was added to close the quantitative coverage gap.** New golden P37 `plink_group_nofast_quant.txt` has **`Pvalue` exactly 1 on 5 of its 12 cells**, beside `Pvalue_Burden` and `Pvalue_SKAT` of 0.82-0.99 on those same rows. The threshold was then measured directly on the kernel, independently of SAIGE: 400 random (`Score`, `Phi`) pairs through `Met_SKAT_Get_Pvalue` with `r.corr = seq(0, 1, 0.1)` gave `p.value == 1` for **55 of 400**, every one with `min(p.val.each) > 0.777`, and **no** case with `min(p.val.each) > 0.8` returned anything but 1 (largest `min(p.val.each)` among the non-saturated cases: 0.7629). POSITIVE and NEGATIVE control are the two arms of that sweep -- small per-rho p never saturates, large per-rho p always does. Link (b) was then closed arithmetically on P37: GENE2's reported Cauchy is 0.978045700202351 and `min(1, 6 * 0.163007616700392)` is 0.978045700202352, while the true ACAT over the same six cells is 1 -- so the printed value IS the Bonferroni, to the last digit. **The suite already carries the detector** and applies it to one binary golden only: `test-invariants-goldens.R:106-110` asserts `Pvalue` lies between the component tests and within 2x of the smaller, which P37 violates on 5 rows. GATE GAP: every previously committed group golden is BINARY, and none of their cells saturates (P6's largest component p is 0.94 and its Cauchy rows reconcile with ACAT), so nothing could see it. NOT quantitative-specific in mechanism -- only in which bundled fixture reaches the regime | **DECIDED 2026-09-05: leave, documented.** Options laid out for the owner were leave / exclude the exact-1 cells from the Cauchy combination / clamp them to 1 - eps; the owner chose to leave the arithmetic and document it. Documented in the `--r.corr` CLI help and the `SPAGMMATtest` `r.corr` roxygen (the Cauchy row is a Bonferroni whenever any cell's SKAT-O p is exactly 1). Two facts verified against the sources on 2026-09-05: STAAR's CCT returns 1 when any input is 1, and the ACAT package warns and lets tan(-pi/2) run, so the min(1, n*min p) fallback is SAIGE's own edit (`R/CCT_modified.R:1` says so). The ledger's earlier claim that SKAT-O cannot exceed both component tests was too strong: the SKAT-O p is bounded below by the smallest component, not above by the largest, so the defect is the point mass at exactly 1, not the ordering. Characterized by `tests/testthat/test-step2-group-quant.R` (unchanged). If the decision is revisited, the gate is the widened invariant in `test-invariants-goldens.R` plus a CCT unit test with a planted 1, and only golden P37's two Cauchy rows move. **PRIMARY SOURCE, 2026-09-11: the fallback is the PAPER'S OWN PRESCRIPTION, not only SAIGE's local edit.** SAIGE-GENE+ (Zhou et al., Nat Genet 2022, doi 10.1038/s41588-022-01178-w, open access as PMC9534766; received 2021-07-11, accepted 2022-07-29), Methods ("Aggregating multiple tests"), verbatim: "Note that the Cauchy combination does not work when a P value of any individual test is unity. Therefore, we used the minimum P value with Bonferroni correction to combine multiple tests when any individual test had P = 1." Both statements in this row stand together -- the code edit IS SAIGE's relative to ACAT and STAAR, AND it is the published method. This STRENGTHENS the owner's 2026-09-05 decision to leave the arithmetic: it is not an undocumented deviation. What remains defensible to criticise is only that the output does not say WHICH rule produced a pooled p-value, which is #127 and not this row |

A BINARY trait cannot reach the floor: tau0 = 1 and W = mu(1-mu) <= 1/4, so tau0/W >= 4. Since #65 landed, `fitNULLGLMM` runs every quantitative solve on the phenotype standardised to unit adjusted variance, so the floor is UNREACHABLE from the command line and from `fitNULLGLMM`; it remains live for a direct caller of `glmmkin.ai_PCG_Rcpp_Quantitative` or `extractVarianceRatio` with an unstandardised small-variance trait, and it is an arbitrary absolute constant in code that #65 had to route around rather than fix. Recorded as its own row on the owner's request (2026-09-05) because it was documented only inside #65's narrative | **FIXED (2026-09-08).** The three live sites are NOT one mechanism, and the fix differs by consumer -- established by tracing every caller before touching anything. `gen_sp_Sigma`'s clamped diagonal IS the matrix: its only caller is `gen_spsolve_v4`, the direct sparse solve that the sparse-GRM fits take at default flags, so the clamp changed the answer outright. Its floor is DELETED, with no replacement. Sigma's diagonal is a sum of non-negative terms, so the only value it cannot take is a non-positive one, which means the variance components have collapsed -- and `gen_spsolve_v4` ALREADY reports that, throwing `spsolve(): solution not found` when the factorisation fails. The clamp was precisely what stopped that from being reached, by perturbing the diagonal until a matrix that is not the model's Sigma factorised; removing it restores the loud failure the row asked for without adding a new one. `getDiagOfSigma` and `getDiagOfSigma_LOCO` are different: all four of their call sites are `minvVec = 1/getDiagOfSigma (...)`, the Jacobi PRECONDITIONER, which cannot change the solution of a CONVERGED PCG -- but SAIGE's PCG stops on an absolute residual (#6), so where it stopped, and hence the answer, did depend on the clamp. They now clamp only a non-positive or non-finite entry, and to 1 (no preconditioning for that sample), which is neutral and scale-free; a small POSITIVE diagonal is the correct preconditioner for a small-scaled trait and is left alone. A `tau` component below `tol` is set to exactly zero by the AI-REML step (`tauVec.elem(find(tauVec < tol)).zeros()`), so a zero diagonal is REACHABLE and throwing at these two sites would have turned a limping fit into a hard error -- which is why they keep a fallback and only `gen_sp_Sigma` loses its floor. The copy at `:2727` stays untouched: it is inside a comment block. Characterization `tests/testthat/test-step1-sigma-diag-floor.R` (fast tier; no fit needed -- `setupSparseGRM` installs a 6x6 GRM and `gen_sp_Sigma` is exported, so the clamp is reached directly, which is the direct caller this row said no test had) green at HEAD as a pin of the defect, inverted in the same commit, and drilled BOTH ways: 5 failures against the pre-fix scratch install (`harness/work/lib-old`), 0 after the rebuild. It pins two controls that hold either way (a diagonal above the floor, and a binary-shaped W, which cannot reach the floor since tau0 = 1 and W <= 1/4) and two inverted claims: at tau = (2.2e-5, 4.6e-5) -- the bundled quantitative phenotype over 100 -- the diagonal is now the model's own rather than a flat 1e-4, the old clamp having inflated it by a MEASURED 1.47x; and the diagonal is now exactly scale-equivariant, a trait scaled by s giving a diagonal scaled by s^2 (measured 1.33e-4 against the exact 1.00e-4 before). **No golden moved, as predicted BEFORE the run and from the goldens themselves**: the clamp fires only below 1e-4, and the minimum diagonal over all five step-1 golden configurations is 0.216 (quantitative `tau0/W`) and 4.0 (binary, `tau0/W >= 4`), both far above it, so the old and new rules agree bit for bit there. Pre-push gate green in BOTH compare modes |

Any quantitative model fitted with `--useSparseGRMforVarRatio=TRUE` between `66a65bb9` and this commit must be refitted |

**Historical note: the marginal column had #41's exact geometry, and it is fixed.**
Between `d65a709e`/`81c95cb8` (2022-09-08, "deal with p-values = 0", DESCRIPTION at
v1.1.6) and `1f637a58`/`576fe839` (2023-09-09, shipped as v1.3.0 the same day), the
MARGINAL single-variant SE used the ONE-sided quantile:
`t_qval = R::qnorm(t_SPApval, 0, 1, false, ispvallog)` instead of `t_SPApval/2`, and
the Firth arm likewise used `qnorm(pval, ...)`. So `SE = abs(BETA)/z_p` instead of
`abs(BETA)/z_(p/2)` for every SPA-applied (and every Firth) marker -- **SE
overestimated** by `z_(p/2)/z_p`: 28.3% at p = 0.1, 19.2% at 0.05, 6.5% at 1e-3, 2.1% at 1e-8.
Plotting the reported p against `2*pnorm(-abs(BETA/SE))` gives the SAME slope-1/2
branch as #41, gated at the SPA cutoff (`SPAcutoff = 1.6449` puts the identity
line's lower end at p = 0.1 and the branch's upper end at 0.2), **but the correct
value there is the reported p and the SE is the wrong column** -- the exact opposite
of #41, where the SE is right and the print is halved. Affected releases: 1.1.6
through 1.2.0; 1.3.0 onward are clean, and so is this branch (fork HEAD is v1.5.2).
Verified rather than assumed: the marginal `p.value` round-trips at ratio 1 over all
22 committed goldens (6,073 marker rows, PLINK and PGEN) and 11 flag configurations
swept on 2026-09-01 (`SPAcutoff` 2 and 0.5, `--is_Firth_beta` at `pCutoffforFirth`
0.05 and 0.1, `--max_MAC_for_ER=20`, `--is_noadjCov=FALSE`, `--is_fastTest=TRUE`,
`--condition`, quantitative). #41 survived the 2023 fix because that commit corrected
the three *quantile* calls and never looked at the *print* two screens below.

**Reproduced 2026-09-01**, to settle which column is wrong. `1f637a58`'s marginal
line was reverted (one line, `t_SPApval/2` -> `t_SPApval`), rebuilt, and run against
the same 128,868-marker fixture at `--SPAcutoff=1.6449`. Result: `p.value`, `BETA`,
`Tstat` and `var` are **byte-identical between the two builds** -- the defect cannot
reach the p-value, which settles the question: on the branch the reported p is
right and the SE is the wrong column. `SE` differs on exactly the 11,932 SPA rows
that are not ER, and the observed inflation matches the closed form `z_(p/2)/z_p`
to 8.4e-6 relative (median 1.189, max 1.283). Geometry reproduces the reported
figure exactly: the identity branch's lower end and the slope-1/2 branch's upper
end sit at `2*pnorm(-SPAcutoff)` and twice the largest SPA p -- 0.1000 and 0.1995 at
cutoff 1.6449, 0.0455 and 0.1163 at the default cutoff 2, i.e. the elbow tracks the
gate. One subtlety worth knowing when reading such a plot: the **ER path is immune**
(its own `/2` at `:799` was never removed), yet ER markers are flagged
`Is.SPA = true` because the ER branch sets `t_isSPAConverge = true` at `:801` -- 393
of the 12,325 `Is.SPA` rows here are MAC <= 4 ER markers sitting on the diagonal,
so `Is.SPA` does not partition that figure cleanly and is a misnomer on those rows.

Two entirely separate code paths -- R matrix algebra over an m x m `Phi`, versus C++ collapsing genotypes into one pseudo-variant tested through `getMarkerPval` -- reconcile ONLY under the correction. Test re-framed from characterization to assertion; the help strings pinned by `test-step2-cli-help.R`, drilled 5 failures at unpatched HEAD, green after|

MEASURED on P58's cell, 384 sets (2 genes x 192), by rebuilding each set's weights with `flexrvWeightsR` and its own MAC from the run's own singleAssoc pseudo-marker rows: the printed MAC counts markers the set zeroed on 174 of 384 (45.3%), median over-count 36 alleles (max 65), median printed/true 2.83x; 79 of 384 (20.6%) would select a different variance-ratio band, but this model's two `null` ratios are 0.9475 and 0.9421, so the SE moves 0.29% and the band is the LEAST of it on this fixture (a model with more dispersed ratios would differ, not measured); and 32 of 384 (8.3%) pass the gate today on the cell MAC and would be REFUSED on the per-set MAC, all of them MAC-ladder sets, p today 0.0126 to 0.78. Worst case `REVEL_lof_MAC1` in GENE2: printed 37, true 1, i.e. a burden test over a single allele feeding the gene's Cauchy row, which is exactly what the gate exists to prevent. The ultra-rare pseudo-marker sub-decision is STILL OPEN and now has a consequence: if the per-set count is an unweighted integer over non-zero-weight markers then any cell surviving `indexNonZeroVec_arma.n_elem > 0` has MAC >= 1 and a gate of 1 is inert BY CONSTRUCTION; if the pseudo-marker is counted weighted, fractional MACs persist (the goldens already show 3.34 and 82.517) and it is not. Implementation gate in #126 **CAVEAT on the numbers above, found 2026-09-11 while checking whether MAC movement requires a zero weight.** It does for the BAND: over P58's 384 sets, band movement occurs in 79 sets and in ZERO sets that have no weight-0 non-collapsed marker, so the owner's rule holds exactly there. For the MAC COLUMN it holds in substance but not to the digit: 197 of the 210 no-zero sets match the model `printed = (unweighted MAC of the non-collapsed markers) + (the set's collapsed pseudo-marker AC)` to better than 0.01 alleles, but 13 carry a POSITIVE residual up to 1.93 alleles, and every one of them has a smallest non-zero weight that is tiny rather than zero (1.8e-3 down to 3.6e-12). The mechanism is NOT identified and must not be quoted: the obvious candidate, #109's dosage zeroing, is REFUTED because every non-collapsed marker in this fixture has MAC 11 to 20, above the `dosage_zerod_MAC_cutoff` of 10. The populations are cleanly separated (no-zero residual at most 1.93 against at least 12 for the with-zero sets, 6x), so the 45.3% / 20.6% / 8.3% impact figures stand -- they are computed from the weights and the per-set ACs, not from this model of the printed column. What it means for the work is that the EXACT definition of today's printed MAC is not yet pinned, and the implementation cannot predict golden movement per file until it is; settle that before writing code **PRINTED-MAC DEFINITION PINNED 2026-09-11, and it makes this row sharper AND wider.** The output's `MAC` column is `MAC_GroupVec` (`src/Main.cpp:2106`, written at :4783, exported to R at :3699), NOT `genoSumcount_noweight`, which is jm-indexed and feeds only the gate/VR/ER arithmetic at :3470. `MAC_GroupVec` is jmr-indexed and accumulates at :2604 as `MAC_GroupVec(jmr) += pr.MAC` inside the r loop **with NO weight condition at all** -- so every non-collapsed marker's full MAC is added to EVERY set in the cell, including sets that weight it 0 -- plus the set's own collapsed pseudo-marker MAC at :3147/:3212. Verified: `printed == (sum of ALL non-collapsed marker MACs) + (set's pseudo AC)` is exact to better than 0.01 alleles on 345 of P58's 384 sets. THE EARLIER CAVEAT'S RESIDUAL IS EXPLAINED and the refutation in it was wrong: the residual is #109's dosage zeroing acting on the weighted PSEUDO-MARKER (via `imputeGenoAndFlip` just above :3007), not on the non-collapsed markers I first tested. The pseudo-marker's own MAC is the one that must be <= `dosage_zerod_MAC_cutoff`, and that is a PERFECT necessary condition here: of 318 sets whose pseudo MAC exceeds 10, ZERO show a residual; of the 66 at or below 10, 39 do. **THE FIX IS ONE CONDITION** -- `pr.w0(r)` is already in scope at :2610 -- but the same unconditional accumulation is THREE lines wider than this row first said: `MACCase_GroupVec` and `MACControl_GroupVec` (:2606-:2607) and `NumRare_GroupVec` (:2617) are accumulated with no weight condition either, so `MAC_case`, `MAC_control` and `Number_rare` are wrong in the same way and by the same markers. MEASURED: weighting rs4 at 0 leaves `Number_rare` at 3 where deleting rs4 gives 2. All four columns must move together or the output is internally inconsistent **PRIMARY SOURCE, 2026-09-11, and it settles the open sub-decision's direction.** SAIGE-GENE+ (Zhou et al., Nat Genet 2022, doi 10.1038/s41588-022-01178-w, open access as PMC9534766; received 2021-07-11, accepted 2022-07-29), Methods ("Collapsing ultra-rare variants"), verbatim: "Ultra-rare variants with MAC <= 10 are collapsed to a single marker"; "the dosage or genotype for each sample of the collapsed variant is assigned as the maximum raw dosage or genotype value among all ultra-rare variants carried by the sample"; and for user weights, "the dosages or genotypes of the ultra-rare variants are first multiplied by the provided weights and then collapsed". So BOTH the `std::max` collapse (:2664/:2686) and the weight-before-collapse order this branch implements are the published method, not implementation quirks -- which also confirms the custom-set semantics FlexRV inherits. CONSEQUENCE for the open sub-decision: the object actually tested is the max-collapsed pseudo-variant, so a per-set MAC built from it ("test-faithful") matches the published method, while an additive sum of the constituent markers' MACs does not describe what is tested. MEASURED on this fixture, the two differ little: additive would give 56 against the max collapse's 54 on GENE1 (4%) and 42 against 41 on GENE2 (2%), because few samples at N = 1,000 carry two ultra-rare variants; the gap grows with the >= 2-carriers fraction, which is the open F5 measurement in `docs/state/next.md` **SCOPE COMPLETED 2026-09-11, and the defect is REPORTING AND GATING, not arithmetic.** A zero weight behaves differently depending on where the marker sits, and the paper's own weight-before-collapse ordering is why. MEASURED on `group_new_snpid_multiWeights.txt`, `--annotation_in_groupTest=lof --maxMAF_in_groupTest=0.01 --r.corr=1`, each arm against DELETING the same marker: (i) zeroing a NON-COLLAPSED marker (rs4, MAC 20) leaves `MAC` at 336.4 where deletion gives 316.4 and `Number_rare` at 3 where deletion gives 2; (ii) zeroing an ULTRA-RARE marker (rs3, MAC 2) leaves `MAC` at 318.6, IDENTICAL to deletion, and only `Number_ultra_rare` differs (12 against 11). The reason (ii) is already right is that the weight multiplies the dosage BEFORE the `std::max` collapse, so a zero-weighted ultra-rare marker contributes `max(x, 0)` and is excluded from the pseudo-marker's value for free. In BOTH arms `Pvalue_Burden` is identical to the deletion arm to every printed digit (2.556854E-01 for (ii)), so the burden STATISTIC is already correct and this row is a reporting-and-gating defect -- it becomes statistical only where the wrong MAC crosses a variance-ratio band (20.6%) or the gate (8.3%). FIVE counters are accumulated with no weight condition, at two sites, and all five are in scope: `MAC_GroupVec` (:2604), `MACCase_GroupVec` (:2606), `MACControl_GroupVec` (:2607) and `NumRare_GroupVec` (:2617) for the non-collapsed markers, and `NumUltraRare_GroupVec` (:2690) for the collapsed ones -- the last is a SEPARATE site from the other four and was missed when this row said "four columns". CONSEQUENCE for custom-weighted masks (`weight:NAME` group-file lines): nothing changes at all unless the mask contains an exact 0, and no bundled custom-weight fixture does (`group_new_snpid_multiWeights.txt` and `group_new_snpid_withWeights.txt` have zero of them), which is why no non-FlexRV golden moves **ATTRIBUTION CORRECTED 2026-09-11: this is NOT "upstream's semantics", as this row's WHAT cell and CLAUDE.md both said.** Upstream (365deeff) has NO per-set dimension: `jmr` appears ZERO times in its `src/Main.cpp`, and `MAC_GroupVec`, `NumRare_GroupVec` and `NumUltraRare_GroupVec` are all declared `q_anno_maf` -- per CELL -- and accumulated as `MAC_GroupVec(jm) += MAC`. With one weight set per run the cell's MAC and the set's MAC are the same object there, so the question cannot arise and there is nothing to inherit. The three columns are also mutually CONSISTENT upstream, which is a coherent design and is why no weight condition was ever needed. Phase 5 of THIS branch widened all three to `q_anno_maf_weight` (per set) but left the accumulations unconditional, producing the hybrid measured above: the non-collapsed part of `MAC` and both counters describe the cell, while the collapsed part of `MAC` describes the set. So #118 is a gap in this branch's own multi-weight generalisation, not inherited behaviour -- which removes the bug-for-bug objection entirely, since that rule exists to protect upstream behaviour and there is none here to protect **UNITS DECIDED 2026-09-11 by the owner: option (b), a FULLY UNWEIGHTED per-set MAC.** So `MAC` becomes a genuine allele count -- the sum of the raw MACs of the non-collapsed markers the set kept, plus the UNWEIGHTED MAC of that set's collapsed pseudo-marker -- and a gate threshold of 1 or 5 then literally means alleles, which is what makes #126's decision coherent. CONTEXT that made this cheap: upstream implements BOTH collapse orders the paper describes, `max(genoURMat, GVec)` unweighted at its :1470 for the default beta path and `max(genoURMat, t_weight(i) * GVec)` weighted at :1476 for user-supplied weights, keeping an unweighted twin `genoURMat_noweights` at :1477 alongside. So upstream's own `MAC` is a clean allele count on the DEFAULT path and mixed-unit only on the user-weights path -- the mixed units are not this branch's invention, but FlexRV sets are custom sets and always take the weighted path, which is why every FlexRV row has them. Our twin is `genoURMat_noweights` (:2069/:2687), already maintained and already unweighted, but declared `q_anno_maf` (per CELL). IMPLEMENTATION SHAPE, all line numbers resolved 2026-09-11 and all of it still to be written: widen `genoURMat_noweights` to `q_anno_maf_weight` and gate its accumulation on `w0(r) != 0`; take the pseudo-marker contribution to `MAC_GroupVec` (:3147/:3212) and to `MACCase_`/`MACControl_GroupVec` (:3155-:3157 and its twin site) from that unweighted matrix instead of the weighted `genoURVec`; gate the five non-collapsed accumulations (:2604/:2606/:2607/:2617 and :2690) on the same condition; and make `genoSumcount_noweight` per-set and weight-gated so the gate, the variance-ratio band and ER read it. NOTE the divergence this one DOES carry, unlike the rest of #118: on the user-weights path upstream's pseudo contribution stays weighted, so (b) is a deliberate departure from upstream there and the bug-for-bug rule genuinely applies -- taken knowingly. FREE INTERNAL CHECK that falls out: under (b) `MAC_GroupVec` and `genoSumcount_noweight` become the same quantity computed two ways (`pr.MAC` against `arma::accu` of the non-zero genotypes), so they must reconcile, and any gap between them is a located error rather than a rounding annoyance **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-11, exactly the shape resolved above.** `genoURMat_noweights` and `genoSumcount_noweight` are both `q_anno_maf_weight` (jmr) now; the five non-collapsed accumulations at :2604-:2618 are gated on `pr.w0(r) != 0` and so is the ultra-rare count at :2690 (on `wUR`, where a zero weight was already excluded from `genoURMat` for free by `max(x, 0*g) == x`); the pseudo-marker's contribution to `MAC_GroupVec` and to `MACCase_`/`MACControl_GroupVec` comes from the unweighted twin at :3147/:3212, computed as `MAC_nw = min(af_nw, 1-af_nw) * 2 * t_n * (1 - missingRate)` from the SAME pre-`imputeGenoAndFlip` vector the weighted `MAC` is. DELIBERATELY NOT CHANGED: the UR pseudo-marker's OWN single-variant test context at :3085 still reads the weighted `MAC`, because that row tests the weighted pseudo-marker; that is why every `*.singleAssoc.txt` is byte-identical. GOLDEN MOVEMENT, predicted per file in `docs/state/prediction-118-126-127.md` before the gate and confirmed: on a custom-weight golden with no zero weight (P8/P9/P10/P21/P22/P24/P34/P37) ONLY `MAC`, `MAC_case` and `MAC_control` move -- no p-value, BETA, SE, `Number_rare` or `Number_ultra_rare` -- and they move to the values the PLAIN golden of the same cell already printed, because `group_new_snpid_multiWeights.txt` and `group_new_snpid_withWeights.txt` have `var`/`anno` lines byte-identical to `group_new_snpid.txt`; so the CADD row and the REVEL row of a cell now print the same MAC (`GENE1 lof 0.001`: 38.4 and 3.34 both become 5, matching `plink_group_nofast.txt`). The FlexRV goldens P58/P59/P60/P61 move further, because they are the only bundled inputs with a weight of exactly 0 **MEASURED AT N = 50,000 ON 2026-09-11, and it is BIGGER than this row's bundled-fixture figures suggest.** sim50k `y_null`, 210 genes, 192 FlexRV sets, 40,196 cell rows, control `35654e19` against HEAD: `MAC` moves on 96.4% of rows and `Pvalue_Burden` on 8.8%, median relative move 0.8% but MAX 20%. The band ratio cannot explain 20% -- this model's two `null` ratios are 1.0892 and 1.0951, 0.54% apart, worth ~0.3% in SE. The second route is `ctx.useSparseGRM = (MAC > m_cateVarRatioMinMACVecExclude.back())`, 20.5 at the default: a set whose real MAC is small now takes the sparse-GRM score test that the CELL's MAC used to let it skip, which is a different variance ESTIMATOR and not a different band. Counted on the two output files, rows with MAC <= 20.5 went 2,722 -> 4,287 (+57%). So on real data #118 is a statistical change to ~9% of burden p-values, not the reporting-and-gating fix the bundled fixture makes it look like -- and the 0.29% SE figure recorded above is the BAND alone and must not be quoted as the size of the row. Cost: +47 to +76 MB peak RSS (one array) and +26% user CPU, of which ~+4.0 s is this correctness change and ~+2.4 s is removable overhead in the per-set `genoSumcount_noweight` accumulation. `harness/bench/NOTES.md`, `docs/state/prediction-f5-118-cost.md` |

Originally: NOT fixed. The candidate fix is one mechanism for all three routes -- have the pooled row (or a sidecar in the shape of `.skatFailures.txt`, #57) record asked / tested / refused, and refuse to print a pooled p-value when nothing was tested. The precedent for the counting line already exists in this tree: a threaded region run prints `SKAT-O cells: N asked for, M evaluated, K served`. This is a SCHEMA-shaped change if the counts go in the row, so it would move every group golden, and a sidecar is the cheaper option that moves none; that choice is the owner's. It should be settled alongside #118 and #126 rather than after them, because both of those shrink the denominator further and would otherwise each add a new silent route **REFOCUSED 2026-09-11 by the primary source.** The Bonferroni fallback that route (a) runs into is prescribed by SAIGE-GENE+ (Zhou et al., Nat Genet 2022, doi 10.1038/s41588-022-01178-w, open access as PMC9534766; received 2021-07-11, accepted 2022-07-29) verbatim (see #86), so this row must NOT be read as claiming the fallback arithmetic is wrong. The defect is DISCLOSURE, which makes the row smaller and better defined: a reader of the output cannot tell which rule produced a pooled p-value, nor how many cells or sets went into it, nor that any were dropped or refused. The fix is correspondingly narrower -- record the rule used and the asked/tested/refused counts, and refuse to print a pooled p-value when nothing was tested -- and the sidecar option (moving no golden) becomes the obvious one, since nothing about the arithmetic needs to change **IMPLEMENTED 2026-09-11** as the owner's option (4): a sidecar, `<SAIGEOutputFile>.pooledTests.txt`, in the shape of `.skatFailures.txt` (#57) and for the same reason -- no result file moves a byte. `R/PooledRow.R` holds the recorder, the rule namer and the summary; the SKAT-O path records from `region_postprocess` (where the cells are tested) and the BURDEN path from `src/Main.cpp` (where its pooled row is written), and `getPooledRecordsInCPP()` merges the two into one table. Columns: `Region`, `Model`, `Path`, `Rows`, `Pvalues_combined`, `Cells_refused_minGroupMAC`, `Rule`, `Pvalue_pooled`, where `Rule` is the branch CCT actually took -- `Cauchy`, `min-p Bonferroni` (#86's prescribed fallback, reached when some cell p is exactly 1), `zero`, or `none`. Written ONLY when a region has something to disclose, so a clean run produces no file and the one-line summary always prints. Route (b) is closed in the arithmetic: `CCT_cpp` of no inputs returns 0.5, and the BURDEN emitter now writes `NA` instead, with the fastTest write decision explicitly preserved (`!anyCombined \|\| cctpval >= 0.1`) so that only the printed number changes. TWO THINGS A READER MUST KNOW: on the BURDEN path a cell whose test returns "NA" writes no row at all, so `Rows == Pvalues_combined` there by construction and route (a) is visible only on the SKAT-O path; and `region_postprocess` is driven TWICE on the parallel SKAT-O path, so `skatoCollect` passes `record_pooled = FALSE` -- only the SERVE drive, which holds real p-values, may record |

The single test of the same pseudo-marker uses the POST-flip list instead (`indexNonZeroVec`, which imputeGenoAndFlip returns and which the URCell record stores at `src/Main.cpp:3648`), so the two consumers of one collapsed pseudo-marker test DIFFERENT vectors. On a `--r.corr=1` run the column so built is what `Pvalue_Burden`, `BETA_Burden` and `SE_Burden` are computed from, and it is neither of the two coherent readings: the flipped vector and the un-flipped one differ by an affine map that the covariate-adjusted score test is invariant to, so BOTH would give the pseudo-marker's own p, and the emitted column gives a third number. The sign goes with it -- the flip's `Beta * (1 - 2*flip)` correction is applied on the single path and not here, so a one-marker cell reports BETA -0.05205 singly and +2.34563 in the region row. The annotation a group file carries -- `lof`, `missense` -- is a property of the ALT allele, and the pseudo-marker IS the collapse of ALT-annotated ultra-rare variants (`max_m(w_m g_m)` reads "carries at least one of them"; its fold reads "carries NONE of them", a burden of absence). So the choice was not between the two carrier lists but between two ALLELES, and the `if(altFreq > 0.5)` branch is gone: the pre-existing un-flipped branch is now unconditional, `flip` is false, and the emitter, the single test and `region_postprocess` all describe the SAME vector. **CORRECTED 2026-09-12 (adversarial review, two independent agents):** an earlier version of this row and of the code comment said the burden now "counts the ALT allele", and that is FALSE. Every constituent marker goes through `imputeGenoAndFlip` in the per-marker prepare (`src/Main.cpp:2746`) and `pr.gnz` is post-fold values at post-fold positions, so a constituent above alt frequency 0.5 enters BOTH the collapse and the burden column MINOR-coded. The collapse is therefore over minor-allele dosages. What the decision actually rests on -- and what survives -- is DIRECTION: `max(w G)` reads "carries at least one", its fold reads "carries NONE", and only the first is a burden of anything. The ALT-vs-minor question for the constituents is the same question one level down and is LEDGER #131, NOT fixed here. Folding remains for the STATISTICS -- MAF/MAC are still `min(altFreq, 1 - altFreq)`, so the reported MAC, the variance-ratio band and the ER predicate did not move, and altFreq/altCounts print un-folded exactly as imputeGenoAndFlip used to return them. **The reconciliation is now exact and three-way**: on the drill every one-marker cell's region `Pvalue_Burden` equals the run's own `.singleAssoc.txt` p on BOTH arms (8.334812E-01, 1.663821E-01, 7.175196E-01 where the emitter used to print 5.758082E-01, 2.634589E-01, 7.021056E-01), and the two arms' `BETA_Burden` agree to printed precision (-0.860935 against -0.860934561967558); `GENE1 lof`, which was never folded, is byte-identical on both arms and is the control that says so. The SIGN follows: `BETA_Burden` is now negative wherever the single row's `BETA` is, with magnitude `BETA_single / dbeta(MAF,1,25)` (0.05205 / 0.0605 = 0.86; the old +2.34563 was neither reading). **The row was wrong about the SKAT-O path being unaffected**: on a BINARY trait `get_newPhi_scaleFactor_traitType` rescales Phi through `g.sum`, which IS the burden column, so that arm moved too -- `GENE2 lof` went 0.166847187 -> 0.1663821, its own single p. Goldens P62 (BURDEN) and P63 (SKAT-O) moved and were re-baselined after two reproducible generations; **P65 was added 2026-09-12 for the regime that discriminates the OPTIONS** -- at `--MACCutoff_to_CollapseUltraRare=700` five cells hold BOTH surviving rare markers and a pseudo-marker above AF 0.5, carrying 28.3/28.3/28.7/28.7/52.5 % of their cell's MAC, so folding it (`w*(2 - g) = const - w*g`) would set that share against the rest and make the burden a contrast between the cell's ultra-rare and rare halves rather than their sum. P62/P63 could not show that: they collapse every marker (`Number_rare` 0), where a fold can only flip a sign. The reason it is recorded rather than closed: at an ALT-major `lof` site the burden counts the REFERENCE allele, which the annotation does not describe, while the MAF filter and the beta weight treat the site as rare -- so a cell labelled "rare LoF" can be driven by a rare allele that carries no annotation. The framing that makes the choice tractable is that the real question is BINNING, not coding: the rare allele at such a site has no annotation, so it belongs in no annotation-based bin, and the candidate answers are (a) leave it [CHOSEN], (b) admit and count on AF(ALT), which puts a common-allele burden through machinery calibrated on the rarer allele's MAC, or (c) warn or refuse on such sites, which removes them rather than re-coding them and disturbs no statistics. **REVISIT TRIGGER, and it is one number:** the fraction of ANNOTATED sites in a real callset whose ALT allele is major. The consequence is BLOCK DOWN-WEIGHTING, measured at N = 1,000 with the default `beta(1,25)`: a single singleton (MAF 5e-4) gets weight 24.70, while a pseudo-marker collapsing 2 / 5 / 10 / 20 / 50 such singletons with disjoint carriers gets 24.41 / 23.54 / 22.17 / 19.64 / **13.62** -- i.e. **the collapsed block is weighted LESS than any one of its own constituents**, monotonically in how many it contains. **This is UPSTREAM SAIGE-GENE+'s rule, inherited with the beta path.** It was NOT introduced or changed by #133, which only made the pseudo-marker's reported MAC and MAF raw rather than folded; the beta path read a carrier frequency before that fix and reads one after. **The sim study's null AND power arms were run under it** (`harness/sim/flexrv_study`, `docs/state/handoff-2026-09-13-flexrv-sweep-rerun.md`), so nothing in those results is invalidated by it -- they simply describe this weighting. Recorded so the next reader sees the question stated rather than rediscovering it from the numbers. Related: #17 (why a custom set's weight is baked into the collapse instead), #132 (the same carrier-vs-allele confusion in the ACAT-V combination weight, fixed), #133 (which made the reported count honest without touching this).

The fix is to gate on the aggregate count itself rather than on a folded pseudo-frequency -- `MAC = altCounts` -- which is what the printed `MAC` already is, and which makes the printed and gating quantities one number. That removes the `altFreq > 1` clamp as dead. It moves no committed golden (measured above), so a fix needs a golden that reaches the regime: a cell with an aggregate count near 2N, which P65's configuration can be pushed to. The characterization test's non-vacuity assertion is that a cell with MORE alleles is not gated as though it had FEWER.
