The sentinel is `NA_REAL`, whose signature is the low 32-bit word 1954 -- exactly what `R_IsNA` tests -- and that payload SURVIVES the arithmetic on the way to `t_Beta = S/var1` (`src/SAIGE_test.cpp:562`), so `writeNumOrNA` emits the literal `NA` for BETA and SE while only AC/AF/Tstat/var print `nan` through the bare stream inserts. The row is therefore byte-shaped exactly like a LEGITIMATE LEDGER #44 declined-effect row (`BETA=NA SE=NA`), which is strictly harder for a user to notice than an all-`nan` row. Any golden written to gate the fix must expect that shape. **(b) SEVERITY IS FLAG-GATED and the row should say so.** `g_cfg.is_rewrite_XnonPAR_forMales` has exactly one writer, `src/Main.cpp:295` in `setAssocTest_GlobalVarsInCPP_indexInModel_male`, reached only from `R/SAIGE_Test_main.R:548`, so the defect needs `--is_rewrite_XnonPAR_forMales=TRUE` AND an existing `--sampleFile_male` AND at least one of its IDs in the model, on top of chrX in the variant file and at least one missing call. Not a default configuration -- but it is the documented chrX analysis mode, and chrX callsets carry missing calls. Also: the two halves are different defects, not one -- the PLINK half is rows VANISHING silently (the only trace is the `N markers were tested` count at `src/Main.cpp:5403`), and only the PGEN half writes a wrong number. **(c) A THIRD call path, not a third instance of the drop.** On `--condition` there is no QC return: the PLINK arm reaches `imputeGenoAndFlip` (`:4722`) with altFreq ~9.2e15 so the fold fires, and `if(MAC == 0)` at `:4746` does not catch a hugely negative MAC -- `:4751` only warns and the run CONDITIONS ON A GARBAGE VECTOR. Fix that path with the other two. |
| 140 | `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1736` -- the region index line is `paste("Have completed the analysis of chunk", indexChunk)` with NO byte count, while `R/Util.R:105` only reads one when the line matches ` bytes [0-9]+$`; and `truncateOutputToIndex()` (`R/Util.R:123`) has exactly ONE caller, `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Marker.R:70`. The region writers `rw.group`/`rw.singleInGroup` are never flushed (the only `flush()` in `src/Main.cpp` is `:5143`, over the marker writers) | **A restarted region run can silently LOSE regions as well as duplicate them, because the byte-count truncation that fixed #47 was never carried to the region path.** The index is appended by R's `write.table(append=TRUE)` and hits the disk at once; the BURDEN rows are buffered in an unflushed `ofstream`. So the two can be out of step in both directions: index ahead of the buffer means regions are simply MISSING from the output with no duplicate row to reveal it, and buffer ahead of the index means #47's duplication. A `kill -9` mid-buffer can also leave a torn row that the restart appends onto. On the same restart `R/PooledRow.R:129` and `R/SkatFailures.R:72` write with `append=FALSE`, so `.pooledTests.txt` and `.skatFailures.txt` are rewritten covering ONLY the resumed regions. `--r.corr=0` exposes `.singleAssoc.txt` the same way. The warning built for exactly this state (`R/Util.R:128-137`, "...will be DUPLICATED. Re-run from the start...") is unreachable on the region path, and `R/Util.R:223` instead prints the orderly-sounding "we restart the analysis from the N+1 chunk" | 2026-09-12, by the flag-interaction hunt; hand-verified: `truncateOutputToIndex` has one caller and it is the marker path, and the region's `message4` carries no byte count. DISTINCT from #49, which is about the resumed run re-running the last COMPLETED chunk (an off-by-one in where it resumes); this is about the output file and the index being inconsistent at the byte level, which #49's fix would not touch. `tests/testthat/test-step2-region-restart.R:37-38` reconstructs the interruption exactly AT a chunk boundary -- the benign case -- which is why the suite has never seen it. `docs/cli-options-and-group-file.md`'s "FALSE resumes from the .index byte count" is wrong for every group run and is corrected there | **FIXED (2026-09-13)**, S9 carried to the region path, three pieces. (1) `flushOutfile_regionInCPP()` (`src/Main.cpp`) flushes every model's `rw.group` and `rw.singleInGroup`; the region chunk block calls it BEFORE measuring, so a recorded size means "everything on disk belongs to chunks <= indexChunk". (2) The region index line now carries BOTH files' sizes -- `Have completed the analysis of chunk N single_bytes S bytes B` -- written by `indexChunkLine()` and read by `parseIndexChunkLine()` (both `R/Util.R`, kept adjacent); `single_bytes` sits BEFORE `bytes` so the marker path's ` bytes N$` anchor is untouched, a run with no single-variant file omits it, and `checkOutputFile()` returns `chunkSingleBytes` beside `chunkBytes`. (3) On a restart the region path calls `truncateOutputToIndex()` on the results file and, when present, on `.singleAssoc.txt`, to the sizes recorded for chunk `indexChunk` (the region loop resumes AT `indexChunk + 1`, #25/#81, so that is the last completed chunk), BEFORE the writers are opened in append mode. An index without counts (older version) now reaches the NA-count `warning()` -- the row's interim one-liner falls out of the fix. An index AHEAD of the file (the LOSS direction) reaches `truncateOutputToIndex`'s "shorter than the index claims" warning rather than resuming silently, the same behaviour as the marker path. Moves no golden (`*.index` is excluded from the byte gate; no results byte changes). Characterization `tests/testthat/test-step2-region-restart-midchunk.R` (standard): the complete run's files cut to region 1 and given a TORN tail (region 2's first row whole, second cut in half) in the results file AND `.singleAssoc.txt`, the index saying chunk 1 at the pre-tail sizes; the restart must print `Discarded` and reproduce the complete run byte-for-byte in both files, on the SKAT-O path (R writer + C++ single file) and the BURDEN path (C++ writer); plus the other direction, a results file SHORTER than the index records, which must produce the warning. The single file is split by MEMBERSHIP (region 1's group-file rsIDs plus its `GENE1:` pseudo-marker rows), because it keys on the marker, not the region -- the first version split on the region name and was vacuous there. Drilled red against a scratch install of unpatched HEAD (9 failures, all on the truncation/reproduction/warning assertions; the complete-run controls passed), green after (31 passes, loaded path asserted). **Still open in this family, recorded here rather than fixed**: on a restart `.pooledTests.txt` (`R/PooledRow.R`) and `.skatFailures.txt` (`R/SkatFailures.R`) are rewritten with `append=FALSE` and cover only the resumed regions; they are end-of-run summaries built in memory, so making them restart-aware means reading the existing sidecar back or recomputing from the results file, a separate decision. Related: #47 (the marker-path original), #49, #116|

Under the FID+IID reading, one phenotype has been applied to two different individuals. | 142 | `src/FlexRV.cpp:41-42` -- `case MAF_MAC: return (t_mac <= t_param) ? 1.0 : 0.0;` -- against the region ingest gate `src/Main.cpp:2751` (`MAF > g_cfg.maxMAFLimit` returns before `pr.passQC = true`) with `g_cfg.maxMAFLimit = g_cfg.region_maxMAF_cutoff.max()` at `:328`; the default ladder is `flexrvDefaultMAFSpec <- "unw;mafbeta:2,5;mac:1,3,5,10,25,50,100,200,500"` (`R/FlexRV.R:19`), and the pooled row is the plain equal-weight CCT over every set row of the region | **Every FlexRV `mac:` threshold at or above 2*N*maxMAF is identically 1 -- i.e. a duplicate of `unw` -- and the duplicates are still emitted AND still enter the pooled Cauchy row, so the headline `p_FlexRV` is not a combination over 192 distinct tests.** Ingest admits a marker only if its folded MAF is at or below the run's max MAF, so every admitted marker has `MAC <= 2*N*maxMAF`; above that bound the `MAF_MAC` kernel returns 1 for every marker, which is exactly the `unw` weight vector. At the paper's default `--flexRV_maxMAF=0.001` the bound is N/500, so `MAC500` is degenerate below N = 250,000, `MAC200` below 100,000, `MAC100` below 50,000, `MAC50` below 25,000 and `MAC25` below 12,500 -- i.e. on essentially every cohort a user will run. Type-I validity survives (CCT holds under arbitrary dependence); POWER and INTERPRETATION do not, and the pooled row silently re-weights the score axis, each score transform carrying 6/12 of its own unbinned burden instead of 1/12. **The degeneracy is computable from the flags plus the in-sample N before a single marker is read.** Distinct from the FIXTURE fact already in CLAUDE.md ("there `MAC25`..`MAC500` are duplicates of `unw`"), which records the symptom on the bundled data as a trap for test authors; this row is the general bound and the consequence for the pooled row | 2026-09-13, promoted from `docs/flag-surprises.md` F5, which was workflow-verified only. **Re-derived BY HAND from the committed golden before this row was written**, not taken from the agent: on `harness/goldens/step2/plink_group_flexrv_burden.txt` (`--flexRV_maxMAF=0.01`, in-sample N = 1,000, so the bound is MAC 20), each region carries 193 rows = 192 sets + one `Cauchy` row; grouping the 192 as 16 score transforms x 12 MAF transforms and comparing each row to its own score transform's `_unw` row on all eight value columns gives **exactly 80 of 192 byte-identical, and they are exactly `MAC25`, `MAC50`, `MAC100`, `MAC200`, `MAC500` x all 16 score transforms** -- the five ladder rungs at or above 20 -- on BOTH genes. Recomputing the CCT over all 192 rows reproduces the committed pooled row to every printed digit (GENE1 0.139077, GENE2 0.907609), which proves the duplicates are combined; over the value-distinct rows it is 0.095698 on 112 (GENE1) and 0.939638 on 111 (GENE2). **So the reported `p_FlexRV` is 1.45x the non-degenerate value on GENE1 and 0.966x on GENE2 -- the distortion has no fixed sign**, which is why it cannot be dismissed as conservative. The kernel, the ingest bound and `maxMAFLimit`'s assignment were each read at HEAD `f5e173a9` | OPEN. Bug-for-bug: NOT fixed, and it is NOT upstream's -- FlexRV is this branch's. **The absolute ladder itself is a recorded owner's decision and must not change** (`docs/design/flexrv-plan.md:236-241`: "`MAC <= 1,3,5` are absolute and interpretable, and rescaling them would invent something the paper does not have"), so the fix is disclosure or pooling, not arithmetic. Two candidates, not exclusive: (a) an N-aware `warning(immediate.=TRUE)` at spec time naming the degenerate thresholds, modelled exactly on the existing score-axis degeneracy warning at `R/SAIGE_SPATest_Region.R:1642-1658`, which is the same shape for the other axis; (b) exclude value-duplicate sets from the pooled CCT, which changes `p_FlexRV` and therefore moves the FlexRV goldens -- (a) moves none. Note the existing `--flexRV_maxMAF != 0.001` warning documents the OPPOSITE end of this phenomenon (the ladder spanning only the bottom sliver at large M) and is silent about this end, so a reader of the warning is actively misled. No characterization test yet; the non-vacuity assertion is that the duplicate count equals (number of ladder rungs at or above 2*N*maxMAF) x (number of score transforms), which is 80 on the bundled fixture and must fall to 0 when `--flexRV_maxMAF` is raised enough to lift the bound above 500. Related: #127 (the sidecar that discloses what the pooled row combined -- it is blind here, because every duplicate row IS emitted and IS combined, so `Rows == Pvalues_combined`), #138 (the other way the pooled row stops being `p_FlexRV`), #86 (CCT's own fallback), #118 (the MAC these thresholds are compared against) **CONFIRMED AT N = 50,000 on 2026-09-13**, which the row could previously show only on the bundled N = 1,000 fixture, so the "any cohort below N = 250,000" claim is no longer an extrapolation from one toy file. The FlexRV sweep (`harness/sim/flexrv_study`, `--flexRV_maxMAF=0.001`, in-sample N = 50,000, ingest bound MAC <= 2*N*maxMAF = 100) was measured per cell on `y_binary_informative_flex192_c10_r1.txt`: the share of cells whose rung row is identical to its OWN score transform's `unw` row on every value column is `MAC100` **100.0%**, `MAC200` **100.0%**, `MAC500` **100.0%** (3354 of 3355 each), then `MAC50` 74.3%, `MAC25` 48.5%, `MAC10` 20.5%, `MAC5` 8.9%, `MAC3` 5.0%, `MAC1` 1.6%, and `mafbeta2`/`mafbeta5` 0.0%. So THREE of the twelve MAF transforms are fully degenerate (48 of the 192 sets per gene) and three more are degenerate in half the cells or more, and every one of them is combined into the pooled Cauchy row. Note `MAC100` is degenerate too -- the bound is `MAC <= 100` and the kernel is `t_mac <= t_param`, so the rung AT the bound also returns 1 for every admitted marker, which the row's "at or above" wording covers but its worked example did not. The partial rungs are the useful control: they are NOT duplicates everywhere, so the effect tracks the per-cell MAC distribution exactly as the mechanism predicts rather than being an artefact of the comparison. |
